PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
a-
^  ENG  4:10 PM
US  4/22/2026

Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
a-

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
ENG  4:12 PM

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequentiy between wards.

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
ENG  "Ф -  4:14 PM
US  4/22/2026

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records  F
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM  •
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
^  ENG  4:15 PM
US  4/22/2026

Normal text -  Arial  - 11+ BILA GOO  / Editing
PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM  •
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
^  ENG  4:17 PM
US  4/22/2026

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequentiy between wards.

I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List. Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequentiy between wards.

Luyện tập Q2  * D  +

File Edit View Insert Format Tools Extensions Help
Normal text  Arial  - 11+ BILA GOO  / Editing
6
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
a-
ENG  4:20 PM
US  4/22/2026

5c8A100%  Normal text  Arial  Editing
6
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the roor occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequentiy between wards.
^  ENG  4:22 PM
US  4/22/2026

The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
^  ENG  4:23 PM
US  4/22/2026

Normal text  Arial  / Editing
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
^  ENG  4:24 PM
US  4/22/2026

opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequentiy between wards.

Normal text  Arial  - 11 +  I U A  / Editing
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
^  ENG  4:26 PM
US  4/22/2026

Ở côc cốc  Home - Google Drive  Luyện tập Q2 - Google Doc ×  +  La  ...

Luyện tập Q2  # G  +

File Edit View Insert Format Tools  Extensions Help
Normal text  Arial  |- 11] +  BIVA GEO  Editing
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequentiy between wards.
ENG  "Ф -  4:28 PM
US  4/22/2026

6
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
^  E  ENG  -Ф м  4:29 PM
US  4/22/2026

7..!
PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
^  ENG  -Ф -  4:33 PM
US  4/22/2026

health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.
ENG  4:34 PM
US  4/22/2026

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
"CityCare Hospital" is upgrading its legacy paper records to a digital "Patient Portal & Clinic
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List" Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.

PRACTICE TEST 2: HEALTHCARE MANAGEMENT
SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
Management System" (PPCMS). Patients utilize the portal to book appointments, view their
digital prescriptions, and download lab results. If a patient is unable to attend, they can cancel
their appointment; if done within 24 hours of the slot, the system automatically offers the
opening to the next patient on a "Priority Waiting List." Doctors use the system to review patient
medical histories, record consultation notes, and issue electronic prescriptions directly to the
hospital pharmacy. Nurses are responsible for checking patients in upon arrival, recording vital
signs (weight, blood pressure), and updating the room occupancy status. Medical Records
Officers manage the archiving of sensitive data and ensure all records comply with national
health privacy laws. An external Insurance Provider System is linked to the PPCMS to verify
coverage and process billing claims automatically after a consultation.
The PPCMS must be highly secure, utilizing Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for all staff
logins. The system must support at least 200 concurrent medical staff users without
performance degradation. As per legal requirements, the system must maintain a full audit log
of every user who accesses a patient's file. The interface must be optimized for tablets, as
doctors and nurses move frequently between wards.

The PSS is a mission-critical system requiring 99.99% availability; even 10 minutes of downtime
can cause terminal-wide chaos. The system must process boarding pass scans in under 500
milliseconds to prevent queues. Security protocols require that all staff must perform a biometric
scan (linked to an external Biometric Database) to log into the terminal. The architecture must
be redundant, with failover servers located in a separate physical building.
Question 2: UC Modeling (1.5 points)

are a primary or secondary actor. (0.3 points)

actors) and a one-sentence description of the goal. (0.4 points)

SYSTEM
I. CASE STUDY
"SkyPath International Airport" requires a new "Passenger Service System" (PSS) to handle the
surge in holiday travelers. Passengers use the PSS kiosks or mobile app to check-in for flights,
select seats, and generate digital boarding passes. During check-in, passengers may optionally
"Purchase Extra Baggage" if their luggage exceeds the standard limit. Check-in Agents (staff)
use the system to verify travel documents, tag checked luggage, and handle special assistance
requests (e.g., wheelchair access). Gate Agents manage the boarding process, scanning
boarding passes and updating the flight status to "Departed." Flight Coordinators
(administrators) are responsible for assigning gate numbers to incoming aircraft, updating delay
information, and managing the daily flight schedule. The PSS must synchronize data in
real-time with an external Global Distribution System (GDS) which holds worldwide flight and
The PSS is a mission-critical system requiring 99.99% availability; even 10 minutes of downtime
can cause terminal-wide chaos. The system must process boarding pass scans in under 500
